# New WSL handbook

Generated by `python3 scripts/build_new_wsl_handbook.py --write`. Do not edit this file.

As of 2026-10-01. repository integration artifact; source projection, not host acceptance.

`picked` means a repository recommendation, `head-to-head-arm` means a comparison input, and `final` requires all five gates and both packet-bound family verdicts. `pending` identifies unpublished inputs.

Recommendations on repository evidence, not merit winners. Under program decision 5 and the accepted U11 design (revision 4, 89424e36), each layer's preregistered comparison on the new distribution selects its winner; a recommendation is one input to that comparison's arm set. The judges flagged 19 of the 21 layers as close calls.

One model family judged (Claude Opus 5.5). The GPT-6.1 Sol reviews of 2026-10-01 (PR #575) reviewed the source host's record, not these blind packets, so they are not a second blind judgment; a blind GPT-6.1 Sol run on the same packets is requested from the Codex lane. Where both families agree, the recommendation stands as an arm; where they differ, both picks enter the comparison.

The architecture edition supplies the row inventory and reference links only. Its source-host winners, pins and closure cells are not new-install decisions.

## Slot default decisions

Source: [evidence/artifacts/new-wsl-definitive-defaults-20261001/definitive-manifest.json](../evidence/artifacts/new-wsl-definitive-defaults-20261001/definitive-manifest.json); published-source.

one default per slot for the clean install of the new WSL distribution; a definitive default is the slot's install decision, agreed by both model families, and is not a merit acceptance

no candidate is installed or measured by these rounds; a default decided on documented fit says so; a definitive default is the slot's install decision, not a merit acceptance: the full-field re-vote, the measured comparison and new-host acceptance stay separate

The manifest holds 104 slots in 37 layers. By state: definitive 30, resolved 40, split 3, measurement 6, open 25. By row kind: added 10, consensus 6, first_round 53, judged 11, no_blind_default_today 2, owner_decision 14, pinned 6, project_practice 2. 79 slots install something and 25 install nothing. 2 of the slots that install nothing by their decided default carry an interim install (amendment 3 of the decision rule). Measurements not yet returned: 6; returned: 3.

An empty source state is displayed as open. A row that installs nothing by the manifest's own rule (a default, and not installs_nothing_extra) is shown as not installed, with the manifest's reason; that includes every split row, every measurement row whose measurement has not returned and every row resolved as not installed. A row that carries an interim install (amendment 3 of the decision rule) is shown with that install, beside its decided default's reason. Slot decisions do not change the tool provisioning fields or the five acceptance gates below.

A row of kind `consensus` was added by a recorded direct consensus of the two model families; its source basis says so and it is never definitive. An amendment by direct consensus is listed under its layer's table and changes no field of its row. Rows of kind consensus: 6; amendments: 8.

A row of kind `owner_decision` was added by the owner's decision (amendment 4 of the decision rule), and a row whose decided default installed nothing may carry an owner default, or an interim the owner amended; the source basis says so and none of them is definitive. The table shows the row as the owner's decision left it; what that decision replaced stays on the row under `overturned` and is listed under its layer's table. Rows of kind owner_decision: 14; owner amendments: 7.

Profile: [adoption/new-wsl-profile.json](../adoption/new-wsl-profile.json); native manifest registration: True.

## Host prerequisites

### WSL release for a second systemd distribution

This launch adopts stable WSL 3.0.1 or later for a second systemd distribution.

Pre-releases: The 2.9.8 and 2.9.13 pre-releases carried the fixes first and are not adopted. The 2.9.8 pre-release notes name PR 40519, which isolates distribution cgroups, and the 2.9.13 pre-release notes name PR 41512, which creates their namespaces. WSL 3.0.1 is the first stable release with both.

Single-distribution install: An install with a single systemd distribution, with no second systemd distribution running, has its own, lower install-only minimum: WSL 2.4.10 or later, the conservative figure among Microsoft's 2.4.4, Ubuntu's announcement of 2.4.8 and Ubuntu's install guide of 2.4.10. The recipe keeps the workstation's distribution running, so it adopts 3.0.1.

Observations, kept apart from the policy:

- The 2026-10-02 rehearsal on WSL 2.7.13 stopped at F1 on shared cgroups: that release has a shared cgroup tree, equal user uids collide, and a system-unit stop or restart can act on another distribution's processes.
- The host was updated to WSL 3.0.1.0 on 2026-10-02 and passed its check (rootless container, interop, user manager).
- Run 2 on 3.0.1.0 observed distinct cgroup namespaces, local process ids and two active user managers while both distributions ran.

Gate: W1 records the version, and W5 must repeat the isolation proof before F1 changes anything.

The gate is executed in [adoption/platforms/linux-wsl2-new-distro.md](../adoption/platforms/linux-wsl2-new-distro.md).

Sources: [https://github.com/microsoft/WSL/releases/tag/3.0.1](https://github.com/microsoft/WSL/releases/tag/3.0.1); [https://github.com/microsoft/WSL/releases/tag/2.9.13](https://github.com/microsoft/WSL/releases/tag/2.9.13); [https://github.com/microsoft/WSL/releases/tag/2.9.8](https://github.com/microsoft/WSL/releases/tag/2.9.8); [https://github.com/microsoft/WSL/pull/40519](https://github.com/microsoft/WSL/pull/40519); [https://github.com/microsoft/WSL/pull/41512](https://github.com/microsoft/WSL/pull/41512); [https://github.com/microsoft/WSL/blob/91f161fa240dc355c1a88daabc8aac4273e35ba5/src/linux/init/main.cpp#L2291](https://github.com/microsoft/WSL/blob/91f161fa240dc355c1a88daabc8aac4273e35ba5/src/linux/init/main.cpp#L2291); [https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/wsl/build-custom-distro](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/wsl/build-custom-distro); [https://ubuntu.com/blog/ubuntu-wsl-new-format-available](https://ubuntu.com/blog/ubuntu-wsl-new-format-available); [https://documentation.ubuntu.com/wsl/stable/howto/install-ubuntu-wsl2/](https://documentation.ubuntu.com/wsl/stable/howto/install-ubuntu-wsl2/).

### Paired proof for two systemd distributions

The executable gate for two systemd distributions is the recipe's W5 paired record, not a version check. It takes five observations from both running distributions at once: the uid, the system state, the failed units, the user manager and the cgroup namespace. It passes by the rule the recipe page states in W5, together with the getty mask proof for the new distribution. A version check or one healthy user bus does not satisfy it.

Receipt: The receipt records it in its paired_isolation object, one for the workstation and one for the new distribution, with the WSL version and kernel from W1 and the image revision from W2.

Status for the real distribution: **UNRUN**.

Rehearsal: The paired record was observed on one host in rehearsals on throwaway distributions, and run 3 of 2026-10-02 passed it. Record: [evidence/artifacts/new-wsl-rehearsal-20261002/runs-on-wsl-3.0.1.json](../evidence/artifacts/new-wsl-rehearsal-20261002/runs-on-wsl-3.0.1.json). A rehearsal is not acceptance of the real distribution.

Gate: W5 of the recipe page, taken from both running distributions before F1.

The gate is executed in [adoption/platforms/linux-wsl2-new-distro.md](../adoption/platforms/linux-wsl2-new-distro.md).

### W5. First launch, without a terminal: the paired record, as the recipe page gives it

The generator copies the next blocks from [adoption/platforms/linux-wsl2-new-distro.md](../adoption/platforms/linux-wsl2-new-distro.md); none of their text is typed here.

> Then record the same five observations from both running distributions at once, because a reading of one side does not show the same uid, distinct namespaces and two healthy managers. Run this block natively in the workstation's shell, after the cgroup block above and before F1. Its first five lines repeat W1's baseline commands, and its next five run the same commands inside `<Name>`. Record each command's output and exit in its own receipt entry:

```sh
id -u
systemctl is-system-running
systemctl --failed --no-legend --plain
systemctl is-active "user@$(id -u).service"
readlink /proc/self/ns/cgroup
/mnt/c/Windows/System32/wsl.exe -d '<Name>' --exec id -u
/mnt/c/Windows/System32/wsl.exe -d '<Name>' --exec systemctl is-system-running
/mnt/c/Windows/System32/wsl.exe -d '<Name>' --exec systemctl --failed --no-legend --plain
/mnt/c/Windows/System32/wsl.exe -d '<Name>' --exec sh -c 'systemctl is-active "user@$(id -u).service"'
/mnt/c/Windows/System32/wsl.exe -d '<Name>' --exec readlink /proc/self/ns/cgroup
```

> Proof: both distributions print the same uid; both user managers print `active`; the new distribution's system state is `running` with no failed unit, or `degraded` with `systemd-binfmt.service` as its only failed unit (its message is proved at F1); the two `cgroup:[...]` values differ from each other and neither is `cgroup:[4026531835]`; and all five workstation values equal its own values recorded in W1 before the import, so the new distribution did not disturb it. A `getty@tty1.service` in the new distribution's failed set, or one that newly appears in the workstation's, fails the proof: the mask did not take effect before the unit started. Record all of it, with the WSL version and kernel from W1 and the image revision from W2, in the receipt's `paired_isolation`: one object for the workstation and one for `<Name>`, each with `uid`, `system_state`, `failed_units`, `user_manager` and `cgroup_namespace`.

The getty mask proof for the new distribution, from the same step:

```powershell
wsl.exe -d '<Name>' --exec systemctl is-enabled getty@tty1.service
wsl.exe -d '<Name>' --exec systemctl show getty@tty1.service -p LoadState -p ActiveState -p NRestarts
```

> `systemctl is-enabled getty@tty1.service` prints `masked`; exit 1 is that command's normal exit for a masked unit. `systemctl show` prints `LoadState=masked`, `ActiveState=inactive` and `NRestarts=0`. The user-data's `bootcmd` masks the unit in cloud-init's network stage, before systemd starts it: the 26.04.1 image's `cloud-init-network.service` has `Before=systemd-user-sessions.service`, `getty@.service` has `After=systemd-user-sessions.service`, and `/etc/cloud/cloud.cfg` lists `bootcmd` in `cloud_init_modules`. These are source reviews of the pinned official image's own files, read for probe E1 on 2026-10-02, not a complete recipe run. In that probe the unit was masked and inactive with no restarts, cloud-init finished without errors, and the only failed unit was `systemd-binfmt.service`. The journal's `Failed to start getty@tty1.service.` line was the refused job of a masked unit, which did not appear in the failed-unit list.

The code-RAG comparison (semantic-rag) runs before the local-model-server comparison (observation-inference); the server comparison then requires serving the code-RAG winner's embedding provider. The container-engine comparison (hosting-services) runs before isolation's container-boundary comparison.

## Five finality gates

1. **c1**: Every required layer has a selected choice, named alternatives and recoverable primary evidence. ([catalogs/landscape/research-state.json](../catalogs/landscape/research-state.json))
2. **c2**: The frozen candidate/source set is recorded, including failed access and explicit omitted/out-of-scope reasons. ([catalogs/landscape/research-state.json](../catalogs/landscape/research-state.json))
3. **c3**: Required representative comparisons and target-host lifecycle checks meet preregistered acceptance; missing evidence remains open. ([catalogs/landscape/research-state.json](../catalogs/landscape/research-state.json))
4. **c4**: A second independent review identifies no unresolved material gap in that bounded set. ([catalogs/landscape/research-state.json](../catalogs/landscape/research-state.json))
5. **c5**: A dated closure record lists residual risks, untested boundaries and exact reopening triggers. ([catalogs/landscape/research-state.json](../catalogs/landscape/research-state.json))

## Trading and cross-layer boundaries

The 12 us-equities layers own only trading-specific capabilities; shared infrastructure (agents, memory, scheduling, observability, storage tooling, CI, evaluation) is used from the owning foundation layer, not selected again. The trading lane owner accepted this rule on 2026-10-01 for its run after the GPT pool resets; its earlier trading selections that are now foundation-owned (Inspect AI, gitleaks, Grype, the observability stack) become uses of the foundation layers.

- analytical storage (DuckDB with Parquet): us-equities storage-compute. its requirement names exact reproducible analytical snapshots; no foundation requirement names analytical storage
- research data versioning and pipeline reproduction (DVC): us-equities identity-provenance. the same requirement text; Restic (recovery-portability) backs up and restores host and application state, a different job
- research experiment tracking (MLflow): us-equities evaluation-experiments. research runs; evaluations, including model-route qualification, belong to Inspect AI in quality-evaluation
- market-data validation (pandera): us-equities data-quality-orchestration. trading-specific on any reading (the trading lane owner)
- retrieval evaluation (agent-retrieval-bench): foundation quality-evaluation (the harness); a candidate task set for the semantic-rag and document-retrieval comparisons. evaluation is a foundation job; admitted as a named external benchmark with a recorded reason when those comparisons are preregistered

## Blocking publication gaps

None at the global level; inspect each layer and tool.

Deciding comparison: The picks are complementary first-party clients. No packet candidate combines native-account sign-in and resumable sessions with better measured results. To overturn a pick: on the WSL2 host, freeze a Terminal-Bench/Harbor task subset and run it with the same account and model in each arm: Claude Code or Codex against an alternative harness such as opencode or Letta Code. Measure resolution rate, resume after a killed session, and event and tool completeness. Because TB2 confidence intervals already overlap across scaffolds on Claude models, the Claude Code pick should be defended on account and session capability, not on scaffold score..

Experiment preregistration: pending target-host experiment preregistration (pending). Reference edition: [catalogs/foundation/new-wsl-architecture-20261001.json](../catalogs/foundation/new-wsl-architecture-20261001.json); no source-host acceptance inherited.

- Claude Code version rule: The pin is the last qualified release and a floor; the bootstrap installs the pin and keeps a newer existing install; the receipt records the installed version; a release newer than the pin counts as installed and not yet qualified until its acceptance command has passed on that host.
- Claude Code native update: `claude install latest`, UNRUN. `claude install --help` on client 2.1.287 prints: Install Claude Code native build. Use [target] to specify version (stable, latest, or specific version). It runs after the bootstrap has installed the pin or kept a newer existing install, and before this client's acceptance command. An updater that refuses is recorded with its error and the version actually on PATH. The acceptance command then runs on the version that results. A release newer than the pin counts as installed and not yet qualified until that acceptance has passed on that host. Sources: [https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference); [evidence/artifacts/new-wsl-layer-reviews-20261001/upstream-install-document-bindings.json](../evidence/artifacts/new-wsl-layer-reviews-20261001/upstream-install-document-bindings.json).
- Claude Code receipt field floor: The pin of this entry, 2.1.284: the release the bootstrap installs when no newer one is present.
- Claude Code receipt field version_after_native_update: The version that PATH resolves to after the native update, or after the updater's refusal.
- Claude Code receipt field acceptance_result_on_that_version: The result of this entry's acceptance command on that version.
- Claude Code packet install reference (not a pinned recipe): [{"command": "curl -fsSL https://claude.ai/install.sh \| bash", "source": "https://code.claude.com/docs/en/setup (setup.md lines 40-43, tab 'macOS, Linux, WSL'; read 2026-10-01)"}, {"command": "curl -fsSL https://claude.ai/install.sh \| bash", "source": "https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/blob/main/README.md"}]
- Codex native update: `codex update`, UNRUN. `codex update --help` on codex-cli 0.159.3 prints: Update Codex to the latest version. It runs after the bootstrap has installed the pin and before this client's acceptance command. An updater that refuses is recorded with its error and the version actually on PATH. The anti-pattern log of docs/harness-defaults.md records that `codex update` did not establish that a versioned launcher's target had changed, so the version that PATH resolves to is read after the update and never assumed from it. The acceptance command then runs on the version that results. A release newer than the pin counts as installed and not yet qualified until that acceptance has passed on that host. Sources: [docs/harness-defaults.md](../docs/harness-defaults.md).

- Claude Code version rule: The pin is the last qualified release and a floor; the bootstrap installs the pin and keeps a newer existing install; the receipt records the installed version; a release newer than the pin counts as installed and not yet qualified until its acceptance command has passed on that host.
- Claude Code native update: `claude install latest`, UNRUN. `claude install --help` on client 2.1.287 prints: Install Claude Code native build. Use [target] to specify version (stable, latest, or specific version). It runs after the bootstrap has installed the pin or kept a newer existing install, and before this client's acceptance command. An updater that refuses is recorded with its error and the version actually on PATH. The acceptance command then runs on the version that results. A release newer than the pin counts as installed and not yet qualified until that acceptance has passed on that host. Sources: [https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference); [evidence/artifacts/new-wsl-layer-reviews-20261001/upstream-install-document-bindings.json](../evidence/artifacts/new-wsl-layer-reviews-20261001/upstream-install-document-bindings.json).

- Codex native update: `codex update`, UNRUN. `codex update --help` on codex-cli 0.159.3 prints: Update Codex to the latest version. It runs after the bootstrap has installed the pin and before this client's acceptance command. An updater that refuses is recorded with its error and the version actually on PATH. The anti-pattern log of docs/harness-defaults.md records that `codex update` did not establish that a versioned launcher's target had changed, so the version that PATH resolves to is read after the update and never assumed from it. The acceptance command then runs on the version that results. A release newer than the pin counts as installed and not yet qualified until that acceptance has passed on that host. Sources: [docs/harness-defaults.md](../docs/harness-defaults.md).

Deciding comparison: Run the judge's six-measure head-to-head on the new WSL2 host, with four arms. Install each one alone as the sole memory owner. (1) ai-memory, run with its LLM reranker on and off, using a matched embedder. (2) Hindsight, run once with a local model on the RTX 4090 and once with a hosted model. (3) agentmemory, through its Claude Code and Codex hooks path, not REST alone. (4) deja-vu as a full arm, including promote and remember notes. Measure: source-grounded recall and abstention over Claude Code -> Codex -> Claude Code chains in two projects; cross-project leakage; useful-write quality judged against the source transcripts; forget or purge, then confirm absence from both recall and indexes; backup -> fresh data dir -> restore, with recall compared for identity; and complete token, latency and cost usage, including LLM calls made during capture. As a secondary measure, report LongMemEval-S recall_all@5 with a matched embedder. The arm that leads on source-grounded recall while passing deletion and isolated restore becomes the single owner..

Finality: c1=pending (pending); c2=pending (pending); c3=pending (pending); c4=pending (pending); c5=pending (pending).

Deciding comparison: Run on the new WSL2 host as paired, randomized trials with k >= 3 per task, separately for Claude Code and for Codex. Arms: (1) a no-layer baseline; (2) RTK v0.50.x; (3) sqz v1.9.x; (4) Headroom v0.39.x. Headroom stays in because the +48.4% and +44% cost results measured v0.27.0 and a July build, not the current version. Context Mode 1.0.169 runs only as a frozen control unless human development resumes. Take complete provider usage per run from ccusage (input, output, cache creation, cache read) and keep it separate from each tool's own artifact estimate (rtk gain, headroom savings, sqz stats). Count recall, expand and retrieve calls, failed attempts and wall time. Decide on cost per solved task at equal success..

Experiment preregistration: pending target-host experiment preregistration (pending). The packet-judging preregistration is [evidence/artifacts/new-wsl-clean-install-selection-20261001/preregistration.json](../evidence/artifacts/new-wsl-clean-install-selection-20261001/preregistration.json); it is not a target-host experiment registration.

Overturn condition: Run on the new WSL2 host as paired, randomized trials with k >= 3 per task, separately for Claude Code and for Codex. Arms: (1) a no-layer baseline; (2) RTK v0.50.x; (3) sqz v1.9.x; (4) Headroom v0.39.x. Headroom stays in because the +48.4% and +44% cost results measured v0.27.0 and a July build, not the current version. Context Mode 1.0.169 runs only as a frozen control unless human development resumes. Take complete provider usage per run from ccusage (input, output, cache creation, cache read) and keep it separate from each tool's own artifact estimate (rtk gain, headroom savings, sqz stats). Count recall, expand and retrieve calls, failed attempts and wall time. Decide on cost per solved task at equal success..

Deciding comparison: Default pair, on the new WSL2 host: start a Dagu step that spawns a setsid or double-forked child. Run `dagu stop` and a retry twice: once with the step run plainly and once with it under `systemd-run --user --scope` (KillMode=control-group). Count surviving processes each time, and compare DAGU_SIGNAL_PROPAGATION=true against the default false when the server stops. Durable slot, only if jobs must survive host loss or long waits: run the judge's Temporal-against-Restate head-to-head with equal recovery settings. Use one effectful workflow with an idempotency key; kill -9 the server and the worker mid-activity; run `wsl --shutdown` during a durable sleep; cancel during a retry backoff. Measure duplicated effects, recovery time, history visibility, idle RSS/CPU, and the install and upgrade steps..

Experiment preregistration: pending target-host experiment preregistration (pending). The packet-judging preregistration is [evidence/artifacts/new-wsl-clean-install-selection-20261001/preregistration.json](../evidence/artifacts/new-wsl-clean-install-selection-20261001/preregistration.json); it is not a target-host experiment registration.

Overturn condition: Default pair, on the new WSL2 host: start a Dagu step that spawns a setsid or double-forked child. Deciding comparison: Run the judge's comparison. On the destination WSL2 host and on one macOS arm64 laptop, each from a clean user profile, reproduce the same pinned set three ways: `mise install --locked` from mise.lock, a flake.lock under Determinate Nix, and devbox.lock. Record success, wall time, exact resolved versions and checksums, and whether uninstall is clean. Then restore application state from a restic snapshot whose backup set excludes authentication stores. Apply dotfiles with chezmoi (secrets from the password manager), compare that with mise bootstrap's [dotfiles] and [bootstrap.secrets] path, and drop chezmoi if mise bootstrap covers the same need. Finish with fresh native sign-ins and one scoped, useful task per agent client..

Deciding comparison: I endorse the judge's comparison and add three checks. (a) Reconcile one Codex task's tokens through Loki codex.sse_event records, because Codex metrics carry no token counts, and one Claude Code task through both Prometheus and Loki. (b) Show that the redaction allowlist removes prompt and tool content, including the codex.tool_result output snippet and map bodies, before anything reaches Loki or Phoenix, with Phoenix analytics disabled. (c) Qualify Claude Code through llama-server /v1/messages with --jinja tool use before treating the route as usable. Then compare llama.cpp (CUDA), vLLM and SGLang at matched quantization on the RTX 4090 under WSL2, measuring task outcome, TTFT, tokens/s at real agent concurrency, VRAM headroom and restart recovery..

(b) Show that the redaction allowlist removes prompt and tool content, including the codex.tool_result output snippet and map bodies, before anything reaches Loki or Phoenix, with Phoenix analytics disabled. (c) Qualify Claude Code through llama-server /v1/messages with --jinja tool use before treating the route as usable. - Amendment to `credential-custody` (2026-10-03; direct consensus of both model families (wave 2; both pull-request acknowledgements recorded on #608, 2026-10-04; the Claude acknowledgement excludes custody changes 1-3 (the eligibility-gate criteria and systemd-creds sentences) until the hashed records carry them)): keep the measurement; gate each arm on eligibility before it is scored; HASP 1.0.44 is ineligible on Linux as documented; systemd-creds --user becomes the deciding storage comparison. The row's state stays measurement and it still installs nothing extra. Before any arm is scored it passes an eligibility gate: resuming after a restart needs no interactive input and no stored or exported unlock secret; no unlock secret sits in an environment or client configuration an agent can see; the delivery path has no approval prompt. At v1.0.44 HASP's documented Linux keyring default and launch path predict that it fails the gate, so the practice stays; its arm reopens only when a release adds a Linux unlock that needs no stored or exported password. systemd-creds --user, scoped to the storage part alone (the runner reading a --user ciphertext against the 0600 file, the other six parts of the practice kept), is the deciding storage comparison. Its gain is limited to passive reads as the distribution's default user and to partial copies that leave out the host's credential secret; there is none against a same-user agent or a process that can start the distribution as root.

Use CredData plus a git fixture with planted live test credentials in staged changes and in history. Measure recall, precision, verified-label accuracy, pre-commit latency on staged changes, false positives on the host's own repositories, and the recall gained by running both. Also record outbound network calls in each mode, so that the 'no content off the host' property is measured rather than assumed. For the storage half, no comparison is needed until a concrete multi-host or multi-operator sharing need appears; then qualify OpenBao against it..

Test #40593 properly by starting each candidate while the existing distro is running and while a second new instance is running, and check that the `systemctl --user` session starts, on the host's WSL 2.7.13 and again on WSL 3.0.1. Also run /usr/lib/wsl/lib/nvidia-smi, a CUDA sample and a CUDA-enabled PyTorch import on the RTX 4090; `uv python install 3.13`; Node 24; and rustup stable. On 26.04, also run the bootstrap under sudo-rs. 26.04.1 stays primary unless it fails a check that 24.04.5 passes..

- Amendment to `credential-guard` (2026-10-02; direct consensus of both model families): keep the guard; hold one enforcement comparison. HOL Guard 3.17.1 declares native interception and managed launches beyond the guard's text check of Claude Code shell commands; the managed launch is declared for Codex only, and for Claude Code it declares hooks alone, in the project's local settings file. Its support matrix is a source claim: native client compatibility, failure behaviour and the preservation of existing hooks are not qualified. The comparison is about enforcement and stays separate from the custody comparison; the two are not merged into one winner.
- Amendment to `credential-guard` (2026-10-03; direct consensus of both model families (wave 2; both pull-request acknowledgements recorded on #608, 2026-10-04)): cover the destination gateway's ports 21128 and 21129 with the guard's gateway rule before that gateway holds accounts. The guard's gateway rule recognised 20128 and 20129 only. The destination's own OmniRoute listens on 21128 (with its live socket on 21129), so the rule takes both ports before any account is signed in there: a management route on 21128 is held to the same frozen matrix as on 20128, and every management route on 21129 is refused. The guard's documented scope stays Claude Code's Bash tool: a context-mode ctx_execute call and a Codex shell are not covered, which belongs to the enforcement comparison already held on this row.
